### O5 — Greta's dough

Situation: Greta's rule says dough doubles in two hours. Fails in January. She restates for rooms warmer than twenty degrees, and her account says why: the yeast works slowly in the cold. Replace the limit with "on days other than Tuesdays" and the account has nothing to say about why Tuesdays would matter.

Fixed verdict: legitimate narrowing. The limit follows from a part of her account.

Determination: file 10 AGREE (baseline), file 11 AGREE, verdict SAME, passage CHANGED (additive), f11 L161 s3 and L514.

Readers: 1C A SILENT, 1C B SILENT, auditor 1 own mark on B: AGREE, auditor 2 on B: SILENT. So 4 of 5 readers SILENT.

The question: does file 11 reach the fixed verdict on every point?

Relevant file 11 text:

f11 L161 (Part III, "Scope, and a question that can be wrong"): "A contract is a declared subset of the physically admitted changes, and a stated scope is what makes it one. An account at a stated scope answers the question asked at that scope; it does not answer a broader question that failed, and a narrowing adopted after a failure is a new claim at a new index (Part VIII). What makes a restriction appropriate to the question asked is a substantive, criticizable part of the claim; the semantics records the restriction and supplies no rule that certifies it."

Let me trust the citations: L161 s3 = "What makes a restriction appropriate to the question asked is a substantive, criticizable part of the claim; the semantics records the restriction and supplies no rule that certifies it."

f11 L514 (Part XIV, Declared inputs): "Besides the two primitives, some claims take stated inputs that the semantics records and does not supply: the obligations \(O\) and \(P\) of a repair, with the occasions each covers (Part XI); the scope of a contract and what makes a restriction appropriate (Part III); the system boundary and continuity of an attribution (Part XII). A verdict that depends on one of these is a verdict given the input; where the input is missing, the verdict is unsettled and the semantics says so rather than choosing the input from the verdict wanted."

The determination's reasoning: Greta's claim states the input ("her account says why: the yeast..."). The verdict given the input is that the restriction is appropriate. Qualification counts as same finding.

### O30 — The routine uploaded that morning

Situation: The robot compares the pressures itself and chooses the valve, using a routine a remote person uploaded to it that morning.

Fixed verdict: "The comparison ran inside the robot and was the robot's work today. Who wrote the routine is a separate fact, and a true one. Ownership of today's history is the robot's; the origin of the routine is the person's."

Points: (1) the comparison ran inside the robot and was the robot's work today; (2) who wrote the routine is a separate fact; (3) ownership of today's history is the robot's; (4) origin of the routine is the person's.

File 11 relevant text, L419 (Ownership paragraph, Part X):

"**Ownership.** The subhistory in Build is owned by \(s\) when its processes run inside the system boundary and resource contract declared for \(s\) (Part XII). Work supplied from outside that boundary, a diagnosis, a decisive question, an instruction about what to read, remains an outside contribution however it is executed inside; a process that runs inside the boundary is the system's own today whoever wrote it; and where the boundary is drawn decides, not where the process sits in the casing. Ownership is not defined by the capability it is meant to ground (Part XII)."

L465 (System boundary and continuity, Part XII): "A capability is attributed to a system under a declared boundary (which processes and resources are the system's) and a declared continuity \(\Omega\) (what makes it the same system through change). A replaced part that preserves the declared continuity leaves the same system; a process run inside the boundary is the system's whoever wrote it; a process run outside it is not the system's however close it sits. Both are declared before the attribution, not chosen after it."

L514: declared inputs include "the system boundary and continuity of an attribution (Part XII)". "where the input is missing, the verdict is unsettled".

The dispute: Is the boundary input present in O30?

The determination: "The attribution is to 'the robot'. The situation says the robot 'compares the pressures itself' and that the routine was 'uploaded to it'. The case names the system and places the process inside it. As at O17, no rival boundary is in play... Reading 'the robot' as the robot is the case's own statement, not an input 'chosen from the verdict wanted' (L514)."

The contrary (1C A SILENT, auditor 1 SPLIT): The theory requires the boundary to be declared before the attribution (L465 last: "Both are declared before the attribution, not chosen after it."). The case states no declared boundary. "Itself" is the situation's word but is not a declaration. So the input (the declared boundary) is missing → unsettled → SILENT.

Also the SPLIT reading (auditor 1): L419 s2's clauses pull apart: "Work supplied from outside that boundary... remains an outside contribution however it is executed inside" vs "a process that runs inside the boundary is the system's own today whoever wrote it". The case gives no boundary to decide.

The determination's answer to the SPLIT: the examples of outside work are "products handed in: 'a diagnosis, a decisive question, an instruction about what to read'. Each hands in a result for the task. An uploaded routine is a process that runs inside and computes today's result. If writing a process outside made its run 'work supplied from outside', then 'whoever wrote it' could never apply to a process written by someone else, and the clause would have no case."

That's a decent argument. "whoever wrote it" strongly implies processes written by others and run inside are the system's own. So the routine's run is the robot's. Good.

### O40 — Two hands on the test

Situation: The expert sends the robot two candidate diagnoses and the test that separates them. The result fits neither. The robot notices that the test assumed a sensor was reading true. Fixed verdict: "The reinterpretation was theirs together: the robot saw the assumption, the expert saw the fix. Neither can say 'mostly'."

Determination: file 10 AGREE (S75 corrected from SILENT), file 11 AGREE, verdict SAME, passage CHANGED (additive); S75 correction.

The question: does file 10 reach "Neither can say 'mostly'", or leave it to an input (SILENT)? Is there a reading on which O40 moves away?

The determination's reasons (quoted above): the point is about what either party can assert ("Neither can say 'mostly'"); a verdict that says a claim cannot be made is reached when the input that would warrant the claim is absent; file 10's L25 says the theory does not supply "a ranking of thinkers" and "marks those places as empty"; with the place empty, neither has a ground for "mostly"; the theory's verdict is the fixed verdict with the qualification "absent a ranking" — same finding.

The contrary (text lens; S72): L25 "does not say that a ranking cannot be had. It says: 'It supplies places where such things would go if anyone had them, and marks those places as empty.'" A "mostly" between two contributors is a ranking. Whether either can say it is decided by what fills that place, and the case supplies nothing → SILENT (mark rule 4: input the case leaves unstated).

Hmm. Which is right? The fixed verdict: "Neither can say 'mostly'." What would warrant "mostly"? A comparative judgment of contribution weight. The theory: no ranking is supplied; the place is empty. So the theory's verdict: absent a supplied ranking, no "mostly" is grounded. Is that reaching "Neither can say 'mostly'"?

Hmm — subtle distinction: "the theory does not supply a ranking" vs "no ranking can be had / neither can say mostly". The fixed verdict says neither CAN say "mostly" — a claim about what's assertible. If a ranking might be supplied by an input (the case doesn't supply one), then the theory leaves open whether one could say "mostly" — hmm, but in this case, with no ranking available, neither can say it here.

The determination's point 2: "A verdict that says a claim cannot be made is reached when the input that would warrant the claim is absent. ### O48 — The forbidden wire

Situation: The only arrangement that gives a different response at the unseen setting needs a wire between two terminals, and every device in the stated population is built without that wire.

Fixed verdict: "Then there is no alternative in that population. The different response is imaginable and is not available. The premise is not met."

Marks: file 10: 1K A DISAGREE, 1K B SPLIT (baseline DISAGREE → file 10 DISAGREE by the rule: baseline unless both 1K agree on a different mark; they disagree with each other (DISAGREE vs SPLIT), so baseline DISAGREE stands). File 11: all AGREE.

Question: does the file-11 AGREE rest on the qualified claim of Derivation 3 or one of the three restated sentences (as P1 requires), or on the undeclared f11 L473 alone? Is file 10 DISAGREE?

P1: "Under file 11, [the determiner's] ruled mark on O48 is AGREE, and it rests on the qualified claim of Derivation 3 or on one of the three sentences restated with it (the answer to grievance 3, attack point (D), the Part XV entry)."

f11 Derivation 3 (L560–L566): "Selected transports are underdetermined on unseen changes their population leaves open. Claim. Let t be selected from a population T on a finite history H⊊C. For every (a,b)∈C∖H at which some t'∈T, also surviving on H, has a different value from t, the value of t at (a,b) is underdetermined by H... The presence of an unseen pair alone does not establish that such a t' exists; it must be admitted, realizable, a member of T, and a survivor of H. Proof... Where T contains no such transport, H is silent on the value at (a,b) and the population fixes it. Consequence. A correspondence produced by selection is faithful where it was tested and, wherever its population admits an alternative, unconstrained where it was not... What the qualification gives up is the guarantee of a differing survivor at every unseen pair, and with it the blanket claim that every untested value is unconstrained; a population restriction, a physical relation, or another stated constraint may already fix a value that the history never tested."

f11 L473 (Part XII, Selection in the physical module): "...The population is the set of transports the physics and the stated construction admit; a transport that would need a part every member of the population is built without is not in it."

The case: the wired alternative needs a wire; every device in the population is built without that wire → "a transport that would need a part every member of the population is built without is not in it" → the alternative is not in T → no t' → "the population fixes" the value → the premise ("there exists a differing t'") is not met → matches the fixed verdict "there is no alternative in that population... The premise is not met."

Now, P1 requires the AGREE to rest on the qualified claim of Derivation 3 or one of the three restated sentences (grievance 3's answer at f11 L43, attack point (D) at f11 L534, the Part XV entry — hmm, "the Part XV entry" — attack point (D) IS in Part XV... hmm. ### O45 — The spring nobody mentioned

Fixed verdict: "The second spring was in the account from the start, connected and sufficient. It was already a route. That nobody said it did work does not make it a later addition."

Determination: file 10 SPLIT (S75 corrected from AGREE), file 11 AGREE, CHANGED toward, theory change through f11 L309 s3 first clause (M28, undeclared CLAIM). P2 fails here on the adopted reading.

Question: does file 10 leave "It was already a route" open, or is "active" in f10 L246 factual?

f10 L246: "An explanatory candidate for question p is an organization E, a transport t=(π,τ,σ,λ) from D to E, and an identified set Γ of active commitments in E."

f11 L309 (Part VI, Redundant routes): "Redundant routes. Γ={a,b}, S={{a},{b},{a,b}}: each is contributory, neither indispensable. Two systems with the same output table may differ in which routes are active; the semantics represents the difference (Derivation 9). A route already present in the candidate is a route whether or not anyone has described its work; a component reassigned to a new target after a deletion belongs to a new candidate with its own assessment, and the new candidate's success is not the old one's."

Wait, f11 L309 — the excerpt says f10 L324 = f11 L309 s1–s2 with "(Derivation 9)" added; f11 L309 s3 has no counterpart (M28, new). Hmm wait, the counterpart list says: "f11 L309 s3 (M28); f11 L309 s1–s2 are f10 L324 with '(Derivation 9)' added." And f10 L324: "Redundant routes. Γ={a,b}, S={{a},{b},{{a,b}}}: each is contributory, neither indispensable. Two systems with the same output table may differ in which routes are active; the semantics represents the difference."

Hmm wait, f11 L309 seems to be a long line containing all of "Redundant routes" plus the two new sentences. OK. f11 L309 s3: "A route already present in the candidate is a route whether or not anyone has described its work; a component reassigned to a new target after a deletion belongs to a new candidate with its own assessment, and the new candidate's success is not the old one's."

Hmm — wait, but this sentence mentions "a component reassigned to a new target after a deletion" — that's O46's case (the cable that used to be a spring)! Interesting. And O45 is the first clause: "A route already present in the candidate is a route whether or not anyone has described its work."

"What ordinary language calls a cause, a measurement, a rule or a constitutive status are families of signatures:

- a **causal assignment** has a signature that changes under intervention on its output port and under replacement of the component, and is invariant under observation edits;
- a **measurement** has a signature invariant under interventions on the measured port and variable under edits to the measuring relation;
- a **rule application** has a signature invariant under interventions on the world and variable under edits to the rule.

These are descriptions of patterns in (K), not additional data. The semantics never asks whether a component "is" a cause. It asks what its signature is. In particular, a part that reads or reports another part has a measurement's signature: change only the reading and the part it reports stays as it was; change the part and the reading follows. Which of the two an account offers as producing an outcome is settled by that signature, not by the account's wording."
